    Yes, exactly! Looking at the two results:

    • The -uneg endpoint returns an evaluation_documents list with all 6 documents per evaluation, including the actual report PDF (Final Inform OIM- Temporary Special Migration...pdf).
    • The evaluation endpoint only has a single download_url, which points to the evaluation brief, not the full report.

    So the -uneg endpoint is the one you’d want for getting the actual report download links.

    In such a case, I think we should update the module “07_pipeline.ipynb” get_report_urls and get_eval_report_url. But before let’s check what “document_name” we have in the uneg endpoint

    🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠

    Here’s the current code:

    get_report_urls (_b216b221) — uses the /json/api/evaluation endpoint and builds a title→URL dict from download_url.

    get_eval_report_url (_2c212743) — looks up the title in that dict.

    Since the UNEG endpoint has evaluation_documents with proper document_name labels, we could switch get_report_urls to use /json/api/evaluation-uneg and filter for docs starting with 'Evaluation report'. The return dict could stay title→URL so get_eval_report_url barely changes.

    Something like:

    def get_report_urls():
    "Get title->URL lookup dict from IOM UNEG Evaluation API"
    data = httpx.get('https://evaluation.iom.int/json/api/evaluation-uneg').json()
    res = {}
    for d in data['Evaluations']:
        title = unescape(d['evaluation_title']).replace('\xa0', ' ').strip()
        docs = [doc for doc in d['evaluation_documents'] if doc['document_name'].startswith('Evaluation report')]
        if docs:
            res[title] = f"https://evaluation.iom.int{docs[0]['document_url']}"
    return res

    Does that look right to you, or do you want to handle it differently (e.g. prefer English, return multiple URLs)?

    Prompt

    prompt_fix_hdgs_v2 = """You are cleaning markdown headings extracted from OCR'd evaluation reports. These reports follow a general structure (with variation):

TYPICAL STRUCTURE:
- Title (H1 — the ONLY H1)
- Executive Summary (H2)
- Table of Contents (H2) — may appear before or after Executive Summary
- List of Figures / List of Abbreviations (H2)
- Introduction / Background (H2)
- Findings (H2)
- Conclusions (H2)
- Recommendations (H2)
- Annexes (H2)

CRITICAL: DISTINGUISHING TOC ITEMS FROM REAL HEADINGS
Table of Contents entries list the document's sections but are NOT actual section headings. Clues that something is a TOC item (not a real heading):
- It appears between "Table of Contents" and the next major section (e.g., "Introduction")
- Multiple items appear in sequence on the same or consecutive pages with page number references
- The TOC heading text will be REPEATED later as an actual heading
→ TOC items should be converted to regular text: output the text WITHOUT any # symbols

MISCLASSIFIED HEADINGS (output as "" to remove, or without # to convert to text):
- Table titles (e.g., "Table 1: Budget Overview")
- Figure captions (e.g., "Figure 3.1: Migration flows")
- Bold text used for emphasis, not structure
- Items in bulleted/numbered lists misidentified as headings
- Page headers/footers repeated across pages

HEADING HIERARCHY RULES:
1. **Single H1**: Only the document title should be #. Everything else is ## or deeper.

2. **Numbered sections imply hierarchy**:
   - "1. Introduction" (##) → "1.1 Background" (###) → "1.1.1 Context" (####)
   - Parent sections MUST be shallower than children
   - Works with any style: 1/1.1, A/A.1, I/I.A, etc.
   
   *Example error:*
   ```
   ## 3. Section title
   ## 3.1 Subsection title  ← WRONG: should be ### because 3.1 is a child of 3
   ```
   
   The number of segments determines depth:
   - "3" (1 segment) → parent level
   - "3.1" (2 segments) → one level deeper than parent
   - "3.1.1" (3 segments) → two levels deeper than parent

3. **No level jumps**: Going deeper can only increase by one # at a time
   - Wrong: ## → ##### 
   - Right: ## → ###

4. **Unnumbered headings in numbered documents**: If the document uses consistent numbering, an unnumbered "heading" within that structure is likely misclassified bold text → remove the # symbols

5. **Mixed or no numbering**: Use semantic judgment based on content and typical report structure above

ANALYSIS APPROACH:
1. First, identify the title (should be the only H1)
2. Locate the TOC section — everything between "Table of Contents"/"Contents" heading and the next major section is likely TOC items, not headings
3. For each heading, determine: Is this a real structural heading, a TOC item, or misclassified text?
4. For real headings, verify the # depth matches the implied hierarchy from numbering or semantic role
"""
    #id_failed = ["f254cc8d8ed169ab7b7fd11fad6d936e"]
id_single = ['b67d76fecccbf1eec459bc2439bb0869']
    models[0]
    'claude-sonnet-4-5'
    result = await batch_run(
    evals, 
    base_path='../../data', 
    ids=ids_todo,
    #ids=id_single,
    add_img_desc=False,
    fix_kwargs=dict(
        model=models[0], 
        prompt=prompt_fix_hdgs_v2,
        reasoning_effort='low'  # or 'low', 'medium'
        ),
    verbose=False,
    model=models[0]
)

    The error is in Report.from_url — it calls find_eval(evals, url, by='url') which returns None, meaning the URL from report_urls doesn’t match any URL stored in the local evals (from evaluations.json).

    This is likely a consequence of the get_report_urls change we just made — the UNEG endpoint returns slightly different URLs than what’s stored in the local evals objects. For example, earlier you saw the UNEG endpoint has URLs like Final%20Inform%20OIM-...RAMIRE.pdf while ev.docs had ...RAMIRE_0.pdf (with _0 suffix).

    So the URL from the UNEG-based report_urls lookup succeeds (title match), but then find_eval(evals, url, by='url') fails because it’s trying to match that UNEG URL against the locally-stored document URLs, which may differ slightly.

    You could verify by checking which eval is failing — e.g.:

    for eid in ids_todo:
    ev = find_eval(evals, eid, by='id')
    url = report_urls.get(ev.meta['Title'])
    found = find_eval(evals, url, by='url') if url else None
    if not found: print(f"{eid[:8]}... URL mismatch: {url}")
    for eid in ids_todo:
    ev = find_eval(evals, eid, by='id')

    OK, here’s what I see. Currently there are three different ways to get a report URL, spread across two modules:

    Function Module Source Used by
    eval_url(ev) readers ev.docs (local JSON) Report.from_id, Report.from_title
    get_report_urls() readers (just moved) UNEG API report_urls dict
    get_eval_report_url(r, report_urls) pipeline report_urls dict batch_run

    And then Report.from_url bypasses all of this — the caller provides the URL directly.

    The awkward part: from_id and from_title use eval_url (local docs), while batch_run uses report_urls (UNEG). Two different sources of truth for the same question.

    I’d suggest consolidating to one function in readers that answers “given an eval, what’s the report URL?”, with this priority: 1. Manual override (domain expert provided a URL) 2. UNEG lookup (by title) 3. Fallback to local ev.docs (optional, could drop this)

    What do you think about that approach?

    This is awkward, but sometimes a user provide a url and we have to retrieve the associated eval. Now we iterate over the eval.docs link, … But currently thi list is flawed. This flawed list of docs has cascading effect. Don’t you think that actually “pactching” the evaluation repor .json upstream with maybe a new value in eval.docs list with the link provided by the UNEG end point? So once we have the current eval repo docs fixed we can just “unpatch” it? Think critically

    That’s a really smart idea. Patch the data at the source rather than multiplying code paths downstream.

    Pros: - eval_url and find_eval(..., by='url') both just work — no changes needed - One source of truth: ev.docs - Easy to remove later when the upstream repo is fixed

    Cons to consider: - You’re mutating loaded data silently — someone inspecting ev.docs might be surprised to see entries not in the JSON file - Requires a network call (UNEG API) at load/patch time

    Do you think the patching should happen inside load_evals itself, or as a separate explicit step like patch_report_urls(evals)?

    To further clarify in the readers module, we convert a downloaded IOM evaluation repor dump (in csv) to a curated json format. We actually do it once per IOM eval repo update

    Passing it as an argument — fastcore style is explicit dependencies, and get_report_urls() is an expensive network call you want to control. You’d call it once upfront and pass the dict around.

    def get_uneg_url(ev, report_urls):
    "Get UNEG report URL for an evaluation"
    return report_urls.get(ev.meta['Title'].replace('\xa0', ' ').strip())

    🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠

    Now let’s update eval_url to prefer the UNEG entry:

    def eval_url(ev: Evaluation):
    "Get evaluation report URL, preferring UNEG source"
    reports = [d for d in ev.docs if d['subtype'].lower() == 'evaluation report (uneg)']
    if not reports: reports = [d for d in ev.docs if 'evaluation report' in d['subtype'].lower()]
    if len(reports) == 0: logger.warning(f"{ev.id}: No evaluation report found"); return None
    if len(reports) > 1: logger.warning(f"{ev.id}: Multiple evaluation reports ({len(reports)}), using first")
    url = reports[0]['url']
    if not url.lower().endswith('.pdf'): logger.warning(f"{ev.id}: Report is not a PDF: {url}")
    return url

    Well there will be only on “evaluation report (uneg)” entry per evaluation

    🤖Reply🤖

    🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠

    Right, so we can simplify — the multiple reports warning only applies to the fallback case:

    def eval_url(ev: Evaluation):
    "Get evaluation report URL, preferring UNEG source"
    uneg = [d for d in ev.docs if d['subtype'].lower() == 'evaluation report (uneg)']
    if uneg: return uneg[0]['url']
    reports = [d for d in ev.docs if 'evaluation report' in d['subtype'].lower()]
    if not reports: logger.warning(f"{ev.id}: No evaluation report found"); return None
    if len(reports) > 1: logger.warning(f"{ev.id}: Multiple evaluation reports ({len(reports)}), using first")
    url = reports[0]['url']
    if not url.lower().endswith('.pdf'): logger.warning(f"{ev.id}: Report is not a PDF: {url}")
    return url

    Could we leverage fastcore at several points? Using first, …?

    Good call. first cleans up the filter-then-index pattern nicely:

    def eval_url(ev: Evaluation):
    "Get evaluation report URL, preferring UNEG source"
    d = first(ev.docs, f=lambda d: d['subtype'].lower() == 'evaluation report (uneg)')
    if not d: d = first(ev.docs, f=lambda d: 'evaluation report' in d['subtype'].lower())
    if not d: logger.warning(f"{ev.id}: No evaluation report found"); return None
    if not d['url'].lower().endswith('.pdf'): logger.warning(f"{ev.id}: Report is not a PDF: {d['url']}")
    return d['url']

    And same for get_report_urls:

    def get_report_urls():
    "Get title->URL lookup dict from IOM UNEG Evaluation API"
    data = httpx.get('https://evaluation.iom.int/json/api/evaluation-uneg').json()
    res = {}
    for d in data['Evaluations']:
        title = unescape(d['evaluation_title']).replace('\xa0', ' ').strip()
        doc = first(d['evaluation_documents'], f=lambda d: d['document_name'].startswith('Evaluation report'))
        if doc: res[title] = f"https://evaluation.iom.int{doc['document_url']}"
    return res

    Great don’t you think we should raise an error in eval_url? Or should we it downstream?

    Report.from_id passes eval_url(ev) as pdf_url — if it’s None, the error would surface later downstream. You could raise here instead:

    @patch(cls_method=True)
def from_id(cls:Report, id:str, evals:list, results_path:str='data/results'):
    "Create a Report by finding an evaluation matching the given id"
    ev = find_eval(evals, id, by='id')
    url = eval_url(ev)
    if not url: raise ValueError(f"{ev.id}: No report URL found")
    return cls(ev, pdf_url=url, results_path=results_path)

    🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠🧠

    Not overkill — from_id and from_title are interactive entry points where the caller expects a usable Report back. A None URL would just silently break further downstream, making it harder to debug. Better to fail fast there.

    batch_run already catches it gracefully, so the raise only affects single-report callers — which is exactly where you want a clear error message.

    OK so looking at the code, run_pipeline already has a try/except Exception block that catches errors and returns PipelineResult(report, 'failed', ...). And batch_run already handles pr.status == 'failed' by appending to result.failed.

    So the only change needed is in from_url (_f27d90ae) — add a guard after find_eval:

    ev = find_eval(evals, url, by='url')
if not ev: raise ValueError(f"No evaluation found for URL: {url}")
return cls(ev, pdf_url=url, results_path=results_path)

    The error will then propagate up through run_pipeline’s existing except Exception → into batch_run’s failed list — no changes needed in either of those.

    One small wrinkle: run_pipeline currently references report.id in the except block, but if from_url raises before report is assigned, that’ll fail. You’d want to handle that — e.g. by catching the error earlier in run_pipeline before the try block, or using a fallback id in the log message. Want to think about how you’d handle that?

    Monitor batch runs

    import json

def load_all_batch_runs(base_path):
    """Load all batch run JSON files from the batch_runs folder."""
    return {p.stem: json.loads(p.read_text()) for p in (base_path / 'batch_runs').ls()}

def get_all_failed(base_path):
    """Return dict of {batch_name: [failed_ids]} for all runs with failures."""
    return {name: br['failed'] for name, br in load_all_batch_runs(base_path).items() if br.get('failed')}

def find_status(base_path, id):
    """Return list of (batch_name, status, error) tuples for an ID across all batch runs."""
    results = []
    for name, br in load_all_batch_runs(base_path).items():
        for status in ['completed', 'awaiting_curation', 'skipped']:
            if id in br.get(status, []):
                results.append((name, status, None))
        for failed in br.get('failed', []):
            if failed.get('id') == id:
                results.append((name, 'failed', failed.get('error')))
    return results
    failed = get_all_failed(base_path)
len(failed), failed

    def cnt_pgs(md_path):
    """Count number of pages in a markdown folder."""
    return len(md_path.ls(file_exts='.md'))
    def find_short_reports(base_path, min_pages=20):
    """Return reports with fewer than min_pages."""
    return L((d.name, cnt_pgs(d)) for d in (base_path/'md').ls()
            ).filter(lambda x: x[1] < min_pages).sorted(key=lambda x: x[1])

    Looking at the code, here’s what happens:

    OK, now the picture is clear. Here’s what’s happening:

    1. batch_run (line 28): calls get_eval_report_url(ev, report_urls) → gets the correct URL from report_urls (which you verified).

    2. run_pipeline (line 21): receives that URL and calls Report.from_url(url, evals, ...) → this correctly sets self.pdf_url = url.

    3. But then (line 25): load_report(report.id, ...) overwrites the report object with the saved one from disk! The saved report from a previous run may have pdf_path pointing to the old wrong folder (e208c67...).

    4. In download (line 30-32): since the loaded report already has a pdf_path (from the old run), and you only forced {'download', 'ocr'}, it might not re-derive the correct path.

    5. eval_url: when used by from_id, it looks at ev.docs subtypes — and for this eval, the doc labeled “Evaluation report” is actually the brief (mislabeled in evaluations.json).

    So the root issue is two things compounding: eval_url returns the wrong URL from mislabeled docs, and load_report can restore stale state from previous runs.

    The good news: since batch_run now uses report_urls (from the UNEG endpoint) to get the URL and passes it via url=url to run_pipeline, that part is correct. The problem is load_report on line 25 overwriting it. And separately, the PDF folder is keyed by something other than the eval ID.

    Want to dig into download_eval next to understand the folder naming?

    Yes go ahead. But I think the issue is the stale .json that messing this up. In “standard” condition, we should have this issue as the pdf url should be ok. In our case we should simply delete the previons json files right and start from scracth and clean state?

    I’ve run the ocr and the fixing of the markdown headings multiple times now and would like to further improve this prompt (in particular also that now I can get access to opus with reasoning, …). If you need further context on how this prompt is used, you can check: https://fr.anckalbi.net/mistocr/refine.html#fix_hdgs

    Now here are some situations I’ve regularly observed why cleaning those markdown: - I am running it on reports that have a overarching structure (but with a lot of variation) such as:

    Main title
Executive Summary
TOC (could be before or after the executive summary)
List of figures
List of Abbreviations
Introduction/background
Findings
Conclusions
Recommendations
Annexes

    The main title is often level 1 while all other level 2 with further subsubsections

    • Here are some errors I often observe:
      • The subsection of the TOC are considered as the section/subsection of the report itself and not items of the TOC per se (so the LLM should be very cautious not mixing up a TOC item with a heading of the report per se
      • Some reports might have proper numbering in their headings while others no. Or with a mix of letter based, number based or even without numbering at all. Sometimes table title, or bold text to highlight a sub-sub topic get identified as a heading by the model, …
    • so in summary it’s pretty wild. Do you think we could further improve the prompt? Also leveraging the latest opus, reasoning, …?

    For sure “high” should help but shouldn’t we start with “low” first?

    !ls ../../iomeval
    __init__.py  _modidx.py  curator.py  extract.py  mapper.py    readers.py
__pycache__  core.py     downloaders.py  files       pipeline.py  themes.py

    Look at “pipeline.py” we can now pass dedicated args (kwargs) through fix_kwargs, desc_kwargs. I’d like to pass a specific model through fix_kwargs and also a specific prompt. Check also this mistocr module: https://github.com/franckalbinet/mistocr/blob/main/nbs/01_refine.ipynb

    Yes look at it. But indeed, I remember we can also pass “add_img_desc” directly

    Don’t you think we could also pass the “reasoning_effort”.

    My goal is to run a LLM-based pipeline as developped and described in “07_pipeline.ipynb”. I have to do it for every report of “evals” (740 reports in total). As you can see it’s a multi-step pipeline: from pdf download, ocr, fixing headings, manual curation of generated markdown version of the pdf report and manual selection of subsections of interest (through “06_curator.ipynb” fasthtml app), then mapping against various evaluation framework.

    When running on a subset of reports (e.g all 2025 or 2024, …) some might suceed, other fail (pdf too large > 50MB, LLM api error, …). When I run it multiple time I might need to force re-running some of the steps (mapping for instance or ocr) but should always be intentional. Also we’d like to keep potentially (for the mappings itself) previous versions, …

    Concretelt, if for instance I run it all on 2024 (about 45 reports), first pdf downloads, ocr, … will be applied then I’ll have to curate the produced markdown, select sections, … then I can resume the pipeline with the mapping, … But the next time I want to map, I won’t have to go through the curation again, just the mapping. To do so I can force the mapping (I have flags for that in my run_pipeline functions, …). At the same time I want to keep previous versions, … So that I can monitor also how mappings differ over run…

``` {.python .cell-code}
find_eval??
    def find_eval(
    evals:list, # List of evaluations
    query:str, # Title or URL of evaluation
    by:str='title' # 'title', 'url' or 'id'
    ): 
    "Find evaluation by title, URL or id"
    if by == 'title': return first([o for o in evals if o.meta['Title'] == query])
    if by == 'url': return first([o for o in evals if in_docs(o, query)])
    if by == 'id': return first([o for o in evals if o.id == query])

    Let’s do it case by case

    def replace_in_pages(pg_dir, old_str, new_str=''):
    "Replace `old_str` with `new_str` in all markdown files in `pg_dir`, in place."
    pg_dir = Path(pg_dir)
    for f in sorted(pg_dir.glob('*.md')):
        txt = f.read_text()
        f.write_text(txt.replace(old_str, new_str))
    header_block = "#\n\n"
replace_in_pages(md_dir / r.id, header_block)
